ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u134	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, I present a report on the parliamentary draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws, print No 451. High Izbo! I would like to remind you that this amendment to the Code of Criminal Procedure regulates, first and foremost, the principles of participation of social organisations in criminal proceedings, the matter of the post office, the issue of exemption from medical confidentiality, the conditions allowing the media to carry out sound recordings and the image of court records, the issue of exclusion of public disclosure on grounds of the accused's important interest, the matter of giving a voice to the final social representative. It also introduces a correction of the cassation in the form of the attorney general's right to file the cassation solely because of the disproportionateness of the penalty in criminal cases, i.e. acts threatened with the lowest sentence of at least 3 years imprisonment and, of course, higher. It shall also introduce an appeal which may be brought against the final order of the Court of Appeal to terminate the proceedings and to apply the safeguard measure. As is the case in such situations, it also introduces an adjustment to the transitional provisions on matters already initiated but not completed. The Extraordinary Commission for Codification Changes after considering this project at its meetings on 13 and 18 May asks the High Sejm to approve this project. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. 93. 2 The Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall, at the request of the applicants, also make a request from five Members of the minority. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 5 of point 1 shall be replaced by the text of Article. 360: Art. 360. § 1. The court may exclude the disclosure of the case in whole or in part: 1. if disclosure could: (a) create a disturbance to public peace, (b) insult good manners, (c) disclose the circumstances which, on account of the important interests of the State, should be kept secret, (d) breach an important private interest, 2) if one of the defendants is a minor or at the time of questioning of a witness who is under 15 years of age, 3) at the request of the person who applied for prosecution. § 2. If the prosecutor objects to the exclusion of disclosure, the hearing shall be open. § 3. The order to exclude disclosure of the hearing shall be subject to a complaint against another equivalent composition of that court. The complaint shall be recognised immediately, no later than 7 days after the transmission of the complaint together with the necessary files. This is the content of the minority proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Joachim Brudziński]]

The matter concerning the content of this article in subcommittees and committees has caused some controversy. There are various arguments raised here. First of all, it's about the attorney general's authority to disable disclosure. Content of the Article 360 § 1 of this amendment would be the following: the Court of First Instance may exclude the disclosure of the hearing in whole or in part, if disclosure could cause disturbance to public peace, insult good practices, disclose circumstances which, due to the important interests of the State, should be kept secret, infringe an important private interest, if one of the defendants is a minor or at the time of questioning a witness who is under 15 years of age, at the request of the person who applied for prosecution. If the prosecution objects to the exclusion of disclosure, the hearing shall be open. The purpose of this amendment is to organise and harmonise the procedure in the event of a decision to exclude the transparency of the hearing. First of all, it should be taken into account that all the conditions for excluding the disclosure set out in Article are met. 360 § 1 k.p.k. are highly assessed, which significantly affects the scope of their application by the court. This therefore justifies the fact that these conditions are not differentiated in terms of the degree of obligation of the court to decide to exclude the disclosure of the hearing. The Court of First Instance should only be guided by a potential threat to a good protected by those conditions, which could arise in the context of the open conduct of the case. It must also make clear that the constitutional principle is the transparency of the hearing. Therefore, in case of doubt, this principle should be given priority. In view of the special status of a prosecutor as an Ombudsman of the public interest, which also includes the concern for the proper exercise of an educational function by the courts and respect for the right to social information, it appears justified to provide him with a specific instrument to oppose the unjustified exclusion of disclosure in a given case. High Izbo! I propose this amendment and request that the Extraordinary Commission also meet on changes in codification. Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal, you're welcome.

In line with the proposed amendment, this can be done at any stage of the procedure, including just before the end of the procedure. In view of the fact that the social organisation is an active participant in proceedings which may submit letters or statements, it should be noted that the proposed change could significantly affect the extension of criminal proceedings. What is important, according to the proposed wording § 3 of this Article, the court will have to allow a representative of the social organisation to proceed if at least one of the parties agrees to its presence in the case. Absolutely imagined is the situation that the defendants will want to prolong the proceedings by taking advantage of this possibility. In this case, the court will be powerless, because pursuant to § 5 the right to refuse admission of a social organisation will be severely restricted. So, in order to strengthen the procedural role of social organisations, a child with a bath can unfortunately be fired. I would like to make it clear that the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is in favour of the active participation of social organisations in judicial proceedings. However, when regulating their procedural position, the whole procedure should be taken into account, including ensuring the efficiency of the case's diagnosis. As regards the proposed amendments to the transparency of criminal proceedings, I would like to point out that the current legislation ensures the full implementation of the constitutional right to information resulting from the Article. 61 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, and fully comply with the standards expressed in the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms and in the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights. On the other hand, the proposed amendment goes beyond these standards, and this goes beyond dangerously. It establishes as a principle the presence of media in the courtroom, without prior consent of the court. The court will only be able to order media representatives to leave the courtroom in specific cases. However, a much greater concern is raised by the proposed change as regards the exclusion of the transparency of the procedure itself. In the event that there is even a very real risk that disclosure of the trial will violate the important interests of the accused, that is, a person who is still undefiled, the exclusion of disclosure will nevertheless only be possible with the consent of the prosecutor. Thus, the legal inequality of the parties to the trial is introduced, and the powers of the judges, who are responsible for the conduct of the criminal trial, are significantly reduced. It is worth recalling here that since 4 March 2016, the entire prosecution is subject to only one politician, Minister Zbigniew Ziobra. It is therefore reasonable to fear that prosecutors' decisions in this area may often be of a non-essential nature. Proposed amendments, i.e. with regard to the exclusion of transparency and access to the media, this is unfortunately the easiest way to create the conditions for a public lynching on the persons freely designated by the Minister, the prosecutors under his authority or the politicians of the governing party. Unfortunately, even the final acquittal of this person by the court after many months or years may [[♪ The bell ♪]] never restore the lost good name. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is critical of the bill submitted and will not support it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u150	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure and some other laws. By Act of 11 March 2016, the Sejm amended the Code of Criminal Procedure Act. Its main objective was to move away from the adversarial reform of the criminal procedure adopted in the previous term. It was a good and expected legal change by the environment, which, you might say, restored normality in the criminal process. Another revision of the criminal procedure is now being proposed and it is regrettable that the amendments discussed today have not been proposed in the course of the revision adopted on 11 March. Criminal proceedings, as well as other types of judicial proceedings and codes of substantive law, should not be subject to too frequent modifications, all the more so with a short period of vacathio legis. If, according to the Ministry of Justice, further changes to the criminal procedure are necessary, it would seem appropriate to set up a codification committee in order to gather all proposals in a single draft. There is a need for stability of regulation to improve citizens' sense of security and confidence in the institutions of the state. With regard to this bill, it should be noted that some proposals raise doubts. One of these is the introduction of an Article. 360 § 1b, according to which the limitation of disclosure on grounds of the defendant's important interest may only take place with the agreement of the prosecutor and, if one of the prosecutors is a prosecutor, his consent is necessary to exclude disclosure. The question of the exclusion of disclosure should only be a matter for the court (with the possibility to file a complaint against the order) and should not be dependent on the consent of one of the parties, in this case the prosecutor. It is difficult to agree with the statement in the explanatory memorandum that the impreciseness of the concept of the defendant's important private interest can often be a reason for an incorrect assessment by the formation of the court, which is an area of abuse and thus undermines the confidence in the judiciary. This claim was not supported by an argument indicating that such a phenomenon is widespread. A similar opinion was expressed by the National Council of Legal Councils and the National Judicial Council. A further reservation concerns the proposal from the Article. 5 a draft law providing for the possibility for the prosecutor to submit a request for referral to him in order to complete an investigation or investigation concerning cases in which an indictment was referred to the court before the date of entry into force of the law. As can be read in the opinion of the National Judicial Council, the provisions of the proposed law do not contain any solutions modifying the Code of Criminal Procedure to the extent that it justifies the introduction of an incidental provision which so much interferes with judicial proceedings. The possible completion of the evidence proceedings, regardless of which the version of the c.p.k., the adversarial version or the current version is conducted, is possible on the basis of the applicable legal regulations. In particular, this article is referred to. 344a k.p.k. allowing the case to be returned to the preparatory proceedings from the stage of preparation for the main hearing in the event of material deficiencies in the proceedings where the necessary action by the court would give rise to significant difficulties. At the same time, we are very positive about the proposed changes in the involvement of social organisations in criminal proceedings, the facilitation of the preservation of images and sound from the course of the hearing by media representatives and the addition in the article. 133 § 2a regulation on the service of pleadings. In view of the entire project, Members of the Free and Solidarni Posel Wheels ask that this bill be referred to the Parliamentary Committees for further work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u170	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, it is my pleasure to present a report on the draft law introduced by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Family and Care Code Act and some other laws, print No 448, the report is included in print No 522. First of all, I would like to remind you once again of the most important assumptions of the bill. The Law amending the Family and Care Code and some other laws introduces two important amendments. First, it regulates the question of taking into account the scope of the maintenance obligation, the allowance in the amount of the parental benefit and other benefits intended to cover the cost of living of children. Secondly, it excludes the parental benefit, the allowance in the amount of the parental benefit and other non-executable benefits under Article. 833 § 6 and 7 of the Civil Procedure Code Act from the execution of a bank account. From 1 April this year. The Act of 11 February 2016 on State aid to raise children entered into force. It introduced into the legal system a provision of education aimed at financial assistance for families related to the upbringing of children. However, the Act does not explicitly determine whether such a benefit should be taken into account in determining the scope of the maintenance obligation. In view of the different positions of the legal doctrine on this issue, it was necessary to regulate it by law. In view of the above, in Article. § 135 § 3 of the Law The Family and Care Code proposes a solution whereby the parental benefit and the equivalent allowance in the amount of the parental benefit granted to children placed in foster care do not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation. This will avoid possible discrepancies in the case law of family courts and ensure that the provisions of the Law of 11 February 2016 on State aid to raise children are applied in line with the legislator's intention to improve the material situation of children. At the same time, it was also considered appropriate to regulate the scope of maintenance obligations other than the provision of education benefits intended to cover the cost of living for children, parents or guardians of children, family homes and institutional foster care. Consequently, the list of benefits which do not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation was extended to include family benefits and benefits granted under the various forms of foster care. The second of the proposals for amendments introduced by the Act concerns the exclusion of the amount of the parental benefit, the allowance in the amount of the parental benefit and other enumerative benefits referred to in Article. 833 § 6 and 7 of the Code of Civil Procedure for the execution of bailiffs carried out from a bank account. In the current state of law, in the event of such execution, the debtor is the debtor, in order to avoid the transfer of amounts to be protected on the basis of specific provisions, i.e. amounts free from seizure, having to apply to limit the execution of those amounts from the bank account. In order to avoid these formalities and to guarantee the amounts derived from the educational provision and the remaining benefits of effective protection against execution, changes have been made to exclude them from the attachment of the bank account. Those amendments consist in the addition of an appropriate regulation in Article. 890 Law Code of Civil Procedure and Art. 54a in the Bank Law Act. On 12 May this year. At the meeting of the Sejm the first reading of this presidential bill took place, and then the Marshal of the Sejm sent this draft to the Extraordinary Commission on changes in codification. The Commission examined this draft law at its meeting on 18 May. Two amendments were tabled and accepted in the draft law. The first was intended to formulate such a proposed Article. 135 § 3 point 2 of the Family and Care Code, so that there is no doubt that the scope of the maintenance obligation is not affected by the funds transferred from both family forms of foster care and institutional forms of foster care. The second amendment, on the other hand, consisted of the addition in the Act of 5 November 2009 on cooperative savings and credit banks of the rules excluding benefits referred to in Article. 833 § 6 and 7 of the Code of Civil Procedure for Enforcement on the basis of a judicial or administrative enforcement title held from an account in a cooperative credit and savings bank. The content of the provision is identical to the content of the regulation added in the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law. Furthermore, I would like to inform the High House that legislative and editorial changes have also been made to the proposal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I hereby, on behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications, request the adoption of a draft law amending the Family and Care Code and certain other laws, together with amendments adopted by the Committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This tool would be a family account that could be run by banks and cooperative credit and savings banks. The holders of such an account would only be natural persons granted non-executable benefits, allowances, allowances and other amounts paid from public funds, e.g. education, family benefits or social assistance benefits. Only cash from these benefits would be paid to the account and the account would be excluded from enforcement. The conditions for starting a family account would be to provide the bank or cooperative bank with a certificate issued by the paying agents containing the bank account numbers from which the benefits are paid. Opening and maintaining a family account and payment from that account in a bank or an account manager would be free of charge and commission. It would also be unpaid to issue an account card and payout from bank ATMs indicated by the SKOK. The adoption of such a solution would further safeguard the benefits protected from execution and would prevent the media from taking over them recently, thus protecting on the one hand the interests of the recipients of those benefits from unauthorised seizure, on the other hand facilitating the work of banks and SKOKs as an alternative to the current need to verify the titles from which certain funds have been paid to the customer's account. It should be stressed that the decision to keep family accounts would depend on a bank that would decide whether it wants to expand its offer for this type of account and on the person concerned who would prefer such an account to be opened. I think these proposals, these amendments would solve the problem and they are legitimate. In this situation, Mr. Marshal, I submit them. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u194	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I present a report to the joint committees on a draft government law amending the law on the promotion of the development of services and telecommunications networks and certain other laws. The initial draft is in print No 323, but the report is in print No 540. The project, let me remind you, concerns the removal of administrative and legal barriers in the engineering and construction process blocking the development of broadband networks and reducing the costs of building broadband networks through, among others. the extension of the network of technical infrastructure operators obliged to make it available to telecom operators who will use it to build high-speed telecommunications networks. However, as regards the legal basis for these changes, it is primarily the implementation, i.e. the introduction into Polish law of Directive 2014/61/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on measures to reduce the costs of implementing high-speed electronic communications networks, which is called the Cost Reduction Directive or Directive 2014/61/EU. This law was also intended to urgently remove major administrative and legal barriers to telecommunications investment. On the other hand, the adoption of the law from the point of view of Polish citizens is particularly important when it comes to securing the provision of telecommunications services to these users, especially broadband Internet access. In the course of the government's work on the Law, it was also revealed that in connection with the Supreme Audit Chamber's report on the control of investments in the location of the mobile base station - the results of the audit were published on 30 October 2015, the audit procedure was entitled "Administration related to the construction and operation of the mobile base station' - it turned out that there would be a need to substantially correct the initial assumptions of the project so that the audit conclusions related to this control of the Supreme Audit Chamber could be introduced. This was the case with the joint committees which set up the Sejm subcommittee. In the course of this work, two types of changes have been made. In view of the results of the Supreme Audit Chamber's audit and the very numerous demands made by the social party in respect of this law and the changes in the laws also provided for in this amendment, numerous restrictions have been introduced, including those concerning. remove from the draft those provisions which have facilitated the location of mobile base stations in wireless communications, and provide for greater and more effective control on the part of both the social and, above all, the state institutions and local government on the level of electromagnetic radiation associated with existing or emerging mobile base stations. The second team of amendments, which was introduced during the work of the Joint Committees, was a team of amendments which were signalled and suggested in view of the need to exclude the so-called law from its scope. critical infrastructure, i.e. one which is related to the direct security of the most important transmission networks of the State and those infrastructure components which are related to defence, ensuring all security of the functioning of the State as a whole. As far as specific changes are concerned, I would like to report them briefly. Firstly, the definitions of the terms contained in the Law of 7 May 2010 on the promotion of the development of telecommunications services and networks, in the form of inter alia, have been amended. Clarifying the definition of the public service operator and adding the definition of critical infrastructure in Article. Point 1 (1). Secondly, the nature of the cooperation between the President of the Office for Electronic Communications and the Presidents of the Energy Regulatory Office and the Office for Railway Transport has been strengthened, indicating that decisions on access to technical infrastructure and on the conditions for ensuring access to such infrastructure for undertakings operating in the jurisdiction of these two offices, namely the regulation of energy and rail transport, will take place after agreement and not only after consultation. This amendment concerns Article. 1 points 4 and 8. Thirdly, the social side has been enabled, I have already mentioned this, e.g. trade organisations, but also other stakeholders, involved in the procedure for issuing decisions to amend the technical infrastructure agreement in the form of an opinion. This amendment concerns Article. Point 1 (9). Fourthly, there is a mandatory provision authorising the Council of Ministers to define in the regulation the types of critical infrastructure, including technical infrastructure, which will be excluded from the obligation to provide information, inter alia. on the conditions for ensuring access to technical infrastructure. This amendment concerns Article. Point 11 of the Article. 25c of the amended bill. Fifthly, the way in which the President of the Office of Electronic Communications and the main surveyor of the country has been modified, providing him with faster access to relevant data on certain types of technical infrastructure. This amendment concerns Article. Point 1 (15) with regard to Article. 29d amended act. Sixthly, the scope of the Article has been amended. 30 mega-rules, indicating that eligible entities, e.g. owners of immovable property, which are not telecommunications undertakings, are obliged to provide the telecommunications operator with access to the real estate, with a clear indication of their obligations, e.g. on property owners who are telecommunications companies. After seven, it resigned from the changes contained in the article. 1 points 19 and 20 on the location of public transport objective investments concerning inter alia: wireless telecommunications infrastructure by linking them to issues related to the emission of electromagnetic fields to the environment and their impact on human health. This topic will in future be regulated in a separate comprehensive bill on which work is ongoing in a special team established in agreement with the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland, in which also the Minister of Digitization participates. The same nature, i.e. the eighth point of the changes, social comments and conclusions of the Supreme Audit Chamber, has decided to delete the changes contained in Article. 6 concerning amendments to the Construction Law and amendments to Article. Points 2 and 3 of the Environmental Law Act. These issues are to be regulated in a separate law, which is proposed by the social side. In the ninth amendment to the original draft in Article. 3 an amendment authorising the authorities of the Geodesic and Cartographic Service to authorise staff to handle matters to which the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure do not apply has been added. After ten, it was added in the article. 8 (2) Telecommunications law new amendments concerning the scope of the performance of the President of the Office for Electronic Communications due to the entry into force of Regulation No 2015/2025 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down measures concerning access to the Internet and point 3 of that Article introduce the possibility to initiate a single procedure under Article. 139 Telecommunications Laws and Art. 30 mega-orders, if they relate to the same parties and the same investment. After the eleventh, the transitional provision in Article is clarified. 12, indicating that proceedings initiated pursuant to Article. Article 28 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Construction rights, i.e. those relating to the issue of construction permits, shall not apply the requirements of Article. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 7 and 8 mega-rules on mandatory telecommunications equipment e.g. built multi-family residential building. Finally, after twelve o'clock, the entry into force provision was amended by extending to 1 January the provisions concerning the provision of technical infrastructure information by network operators. In conclusion, I would like to say that the work on this law lasted for an extremely long time, and that is because we had a very deep conviction that it was necessary to take into account both the demands and audit conclusions expressed in the document published by the Supreme Audit Chamber and the numerous requests of associations, social parties, which were submitted during the work on the Act. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u198	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I am pleased to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on the Promotion of Telecommunications Services and Networks and some other laws. The proposed law aims mainly at implementing Directive 2014/61 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on measures to reduce the costs of implementing high-speed electronic communications networks. The proposed regulation also aims to urgently remove major administrative and legal barriers to telecommunications investment. The adoption of the law is necessary to secure the provision of telecommunications services to end-users, in particular broadband Internet access and wireless communications. It is also important to prepare and implement projects in the first recruitment of the Operational Programme, in which more than EUR 1 million is available for the construction of the infrastructure. In order to receive these funds, the requirements for spending should be met, which will not be possible without removing barriers and accelerating investment. The need for change is also due to the call for the removal of investment barriers to telecommunications investments, notably those identified by working groups in the Memorandum on cooperation for the construction and development of passive broadband infrastructure. In addition, the legal arrangements provided for in this draft law implement the recommendations resulting from the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber and aim to bring Polish law into line with the Cost Reduction Directive. Consequently, it is not possible to take, using existing legal solutions and institutions, alternative measures to the adoption of the proposed law to achieve the stated objectives. Broadband infrastructure is the cornerstone of the Digital Single Market and is a prerequisite for competitiveness. The vast part of the high investment costs is due to inefficient solutions used in the investment process. As each problem area is linked to a specific stage of the broadband deployment process, the combined resolution of these problems will help to achieve coherent and mutually reinforcing actions. The measures proposed in the Act to increase the efficiency of the use of existing infrastructure and to reduce the cost of new engineering and construction works and obstacles to their implementation should make an important contribution to ensuring the rapid and wide deployment of high-speed telecommunications networks while maintaining effective competition. It is therefore crucial to reduce investment costs. In relation to the original text of the draft law, the draft wording contains the solutions introduced in the agreement with the Joint Committee of the Government and the Territorial Government and received a positive opinion from the Group. The Information Society. The project was the subject of work in committees and subcommittees. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will support the bill submitted to the High House, as amended in committee work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It also aims to remove administrative and legal barriers that often block telecommunications investments. Ladies and gentlemen! We will all agree that the development of ICT networks is a key issue for every development-oriented country. The ICT network is not only - as it sounded from this speaker - the Internet, but also mobile networks and other networks that have been excluded from critical infrastructure, such as networks of supervision of often key infrastructure elements. Of course, as far as the Internet is concerned, this is a significant help for a private user in many areas of life and we should strive to ensure that all people in our country have access to the network as easily as possible and as cheaply as possible. This amendment introduces, among others, changes leading to an extension of the network of technical infrastructure operators obliged to make it available to telecommunications operators who will use it for the construction of high-speed telecommunications networks. Another change aimed at improving the expansion and construction of new network infrastructure is the obligation on construction companies to equip multi-family housing as well as public utility for telecommunications installations if they are not equipped with it and are subject to expansion, reconstruction or reconstruction. The changes also concern the coordination of works in such a way that the telecommunications company can start the construction of the network at the same place where it was started by another entity carrying out its public investment. As far as other changes are concerned, I'll replace them. the introduction of mandatory disclosure of part of the data, as discussed, the creation of a separate information point for telecommunications, which will be run by the President of the Office for Electronic Communications. The purpose of this item will be to provide information on procedures and formalities in the telecommunications information process and information on investment plans. Another amendment concerns the shortening of the time limits for deciding on the location of the road lane. What is very reasonable is that, at the reasoned request of local government units, electromagnetic emissions can be checked from radio and navigational or radio-location installations. To sum up, ladies and gentlemen, the changes made are designed to improve the conditions for the development of ICT networks, as well as to introduce a number of provisions that oblige telecoms companies to cooperate in the construction and expansion of networks and to bridge some of the administrative barriers that slow down such investments. We already have World Youth Day in July, and when it comes to organising these days in order to secure this event in access both to the Internet and to the mobile network, this is the It's quite a challenge. The aim of the proposed legislation is also to urgently remove major administrative and legal barriers to telecommunications investment. In particular, the rules adopted will allow the provision of telecommunications services to end-users, in particular broadband Internet access and wireless communications services. You don't need to be a specialist to see the huge potential that stands and waits to be developed for the benefit of the state and citizens. A significant part of this untapped potential is at the level of network infrastructure. To exploit this potential, you should, among others. increase the efficiency of the use of existing broadly understood infrastructure, reduce the cost of new engineering and construction work and reduce the burden of bureaucratic obstacles to the performance of work in such a way as to encourage investment by the private sector as well. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u228	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Maybe I'll start with this broadest question, because it's gonna take us the most time here, it's gonna take us the preliminaries of the bill on the health of the population and its procedure. As an introduction, it must be stated that the existing legislation establishes a system of protection against electromagnetic fields in such a way that, first, environmental law imposes an obligation to carry out measurements of electromagnetic fields. This obligation shall apply to measurements carried out by an accredited laboratory. Secondly, prior to the start of operation of the installation and any substantial change thereof, the environmental authority should be notified and the results of the measurements attached and the environmental authority may object within 30 days and prevent the operation of the installation. The results are also presented to the Regional Environmental Inspector and the Regional Health Inspector. In Poland there are some of the most restrictive levels of electromagnetic fields, reaching up to 100 times the standards, not 100 times the number of times the number of times the number of other countries, depending on the type of frequency. Our submission aims to complement this protection system with additional solutions, which, according to the Supreme Audit Chamber report, will provide better social control and better state control of electromagnetic radiation emissions and, above all, provide better information to communities about local electromagnetic radiation conditions. In order to prepare these legislative solutions, which were triggered by the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber published at the time of the deep work on the changes in the Act - later these needs were also reported by the environment dealing with electromagnetic radiation, the social environment - the Minister of Digitization entrusted the Institute of Communications - the State Research Institute with the task of carrying out pilot research and analysis of acceptable levels of PEM, electromagnetic radiation, I will say: PEM, including the analysis of the impact of PEM on human health, in cooperation with the social environment of the College of Medicum of the Jagiellonian University, as well as, in the framework of this pilotage, measurement and audit of base station documentation in the selected area of pilot studies, i.e. in Rzeszów and Krakow, in order to allow a supplement, and in some cases even to formulate solutions, legislative proposals. Originally, it seemed that we could balance these activities, but at the last meeting of the team mentioned here, the team acting under the aegis of the President's office, the social side itself came to the conclusion that a significant proportion of the information needed to write a good bill would only be available after the completion of these pilot actions, controls and pilot studies, and after a health check by the College of Medicum. In terms of subjective regulation, which is being prepared and the team meets each month, working on specific assumptions, there are both telecom operators - in terms of providing information on the results of measurements from installations and equipment emitting PEM at the request of the owner of the property, informing about radio installations in public buildings and located in the immediate vicinity of institutions aiming at the care of children as well as operators of broadcasting radio and television stations, as well as digital television, which are also the source of electromagnetic radiation - to a similar extent. Local government units - to publish information on public Internet access points and on submitted building applications. Government administration - here we are planning a significant extension of control powers for environmental control authorities and for the Office for Electronic Communications. Next: equipment vendors and service providers - to inform about electromagnetic radiation emissions by their devices, distributors and equipment manufacturers - to the same extent. The Vacatio legis of this law will vary from 3 to 6 months, as some of them simply require the organisation of some kind of procedure and institution, staff and financial strengthening of these institutions. On the other hand, with regard to this regulation - I am narrowing this message strongly - there will be an increase in social control by extending the circle of entities authorised to request control, an obligation for environmental authorities to publish in the BIP the notification of installations, the creation of procedures allowing the social side to better exercise the right to access environmental information, the obligation on owners of installations and equipment to provide information on the results of measurements to each request of property owners in terms of impact. Further: to increase the powers of control of state authorities, i.e. extending the procedural powers of environmental authorities and the President of UKE to control compliance by traders with the PEM rules. These checks can be undertaken at the request of the social party and, most importantly, this is not the result of changes made to the Business Act already nice a few years ago, they will be able to take place without prior notice of the planned inspection. At the moment there is a provision that requires a notice of inspection 7 days before the planned inspection. This is obviously not conducive to the transparency of the results of this audit. We then plan to extend the catalogue of sanctions, including financial sanctions, applicable to entrepreneurs who do not comply with the PEM rules, including the exclusion of the base station where electromagnetic radiation rules have been violated, entrusting the Institute with the obligations to carry out control measurements of electromagnetic fields emitted by base stations, and devices and effects of these stations on human health and the environment in cooperation with the College Medicum, to develop and publish and to certify the Polish Accreditation Centre's measurement methodologies, the principles for assessing results, including the aggregation of transmitter emissions at stations. Of course, there will be a number of information and education obligations, including the obligation for local authorities to publish information about the location of public Internet access points, which are usually radio-based - here in Warsaw we also use them - the obligation to equip radio equipment with a simple mechanism to exclude wireless Internet access, the obligation for distributors of equipment to include relevant information in the instructions for use on radiation emissions and recommendations for safe use in the Polish language, the obligation for sellers and persons installing equipment in private premises to inform in a clear and legible way about the existence of radiation, ways to minimise exposure to radiation, the obligation on manufacturers and distributors of devices emitting electromagnetic fields to include instructions for the safe use of communications and in the Polish language, the obligation for sellers and persons installing equipment in private premises to provide information in a clear and legible way about the existence of radiation, the obligation of providers of services to ensure that, at the request of a mobile phone, access devices restricting the exposure of the head, i.e. headphone sets, the obligation to inform in advertising about the recommendation to use accessories limiting the exposure of head to wave emissions, the obligation to inform the directors of institutions whose purpose is to care for a child under the age of 3 and the kindergartens about a mobile base station, about radio and television transmitters located or planned to be carried out in the vicinity of such an establishment and the obligation to provide information on the results of measurements. The deadline for submitting this law to the marshal's staff depends on the social side. Until recently, we were convinced that this would happen by the end of this year, and already the last meeting with the social side showed - and this was raised by the social side - that both pilot studies and tests on the effects of electromagnetic radiation on human organisms, including in particular the carrying out of studies on persons who report that they are sensitive to electromagnetic radiation, require that this be meaningful and good results, quite a long time, so that the preparation of this law can take the social side, the government side and the participating business party at least until the end of this year, with an indication of the 1st quarter of next year as the deadline for the submission of regulations. At the last meeting of the joint team, which I have the honour to chair with the representative of the President's Office of the Republic of Poland, this deadline was considered realistic to achieve and ensure the credibility of the research results achieved. As regards the answer to the questions that have been raised here, several of them were those raised also during the work on the bill in committee, among others. the issue of reducing the budgets of local government units as a result of new obligations imposed on them. Of course, at this moment in this form, the law on support for the development of services and telecommunications networks provides for new obligations concerning local government units. Following the adoption of this new Health Act, the residents of these obligations will also come to the State administration and in both cases the costs which local government units will have to bear in the performance of public interest tasks will have to be borne. However, these costs could not be valued. There have been meetings of the joint government and local government committees on which we have tried to optimize, value, objectify the information that is related to construction procedures, in this case partially depleted, but partly new obligations. No such data could be provided. We have therefore agreed with the local government that, as part of the ex post impact assessment work, such information will be presented on a regular basis which will show the impact on the costs incurred by local government units. The attempt to write adequately in the Act was abandoned at the stage of government work due to the fact that, as indicated by the legal commission during government work, the validity of the provision which requires the drawing up of the OSR ex post, including the OSR ex post, must be provided with information on the costs incurred. The local government, of course, raises this argument repeatedly. I would add that the Minister of Digitization did not object to such a provision in the Act, but it was found to be legislatively incorrect because of its existence in other legal provisions. Next, the question of adding the strategic documents of the municipality necessary to decide on the costs of infrastructure transfer. There we have designed a number of documents which are such that they are not only official documents of the municipality, but also precise and can be concluded from them what the costs of infrastructure and infrastructure translation will be. On the other hand, the social side, the local government, requested that the development strategy of the municipality be added. However, this document is of a planning nature, more strategic nature, and it is difficult to conclude what specific commitments are. As regards the costs of the carry-over, the road manager knows these costs and is able to estimate them on the basis of the costs known to him from the operation of the road and, in the decision he takes, indicate those costs. As far as the fees for the technology channel are concerned, here the question is whether, if the indicators in the decision of the local government authority concerning the channel fee change, because the law sets only the maximum fees, there is an automatic change in the fees for the entrepreneur. No, it is an administrative decision and every time any change makes it necessary to make a new administrative decision, because it refers both to the length of the channel used by the trader and to the new rate that can be variable. Costs. Here, too, the question was whether the costs of construction of infrastructure as a result of the adoption of the law would decrease and whether this would improve the implementation of the Operational Programme. Yes, of course, in principle the costs should decrease significantly. First of all, the most important effect of this law should be to accelerate the implementation of these investments. I would like to remind you that the Digital Poland Operational Programme has been in force since 2014, so we already have a 2-year delay in its physical implementation, in the implementation of the investment. We do not have much time left to build infrastructure, which this time is not a long-distance infrastructure, which is built very quickly, but local infrastructure, built in excluded areas, areas of white spots, and this infrastructure is built by nature for a very long time. So that all the facilitations introduced by the law, especially as regards these facilities, which lasted and concern wired infrastructure, should greatly improve the investment from the Operational Programme, as well as all other investments carried out by telecommunications operators, which could allow us to leave second or third from the end, depending on the year, the place in all rankings of the European Union. On the other hand, as regards these white spots, areas where the cost-effectiveness of building wired networks will still be too low for operators to be interested in the construction of wired networks in these places, the Operational Programme As a rule, mobile networks are intended for such areas where comparable and sometimes even higher speeds are available at the moment than for wired networks. This allows very efficient, fast connection of white spots to the network. Let me remind you that in Poland 39% citizens of our country live in areas where population density is lower than 100 per km. These are areas that are absolutely uneconomical for any telecommunications business and which are inefficient or low cost in case of maintaining a network built up from EU funds. We must therefore improve the efficiency of this construction by improving this by reducing the costs of the investment itself, expanding it and subsequently maintaining these networks, reducing the obligations imposed on operators. Thus, in the case of networks in the most excluded areas, the reduction of these costs will not yet be as desired, and the effect of the Operational Programme will not cover the whole country, excluding or filling all white spots with efficient broadband infrastructure. The last question I took note of was the gas and energy networks. While considering the possibility of this law affecting gas and energy networks, I see only one point of this law concerning the use of gas and energy infrastructure by telecommunications operators. At the same time, I believe that by the provisions that have been introduced, which restrict the use of this gas and energy network, if it is a critical infrastructure - and for today, without having a regulation by the Council of Ministers on this matter, because it is a parliamentary amendment, we do not know which part of this network will be regarded as a critical infrastructure - we can de facto even worsen the condition of the gas and energy networks, because this is the use of paid and many entrepreneurs, in particular energy, achieve quite good revenues from making their networks available to telecom operators. This is an important item in their budgets and this activity in the field of energy and gas networks is constantly developing. Therefore, the construction of telecommunications networks in the energy infrastructure does not burden it, but, on the contrary, results in additional revenues, which are very important in these precisely excluded areas, where gas and energy networks also require very large investments at a huge unit cost, on the citizen. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u232	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the government draft law on the rules for determining the remuneration of persons running certain companies. The project entered the Sejm on May 10th. On May 13, he was assigned to his first reading. The first reading took place at the meeting of the Sejm on 19 May and the draft was submitted to work in committee. The bill aims primarily at changing the approach to the principles of wage formation in commercial companies with the participation of the State Treasury, local government units and their associations, as well as state and municipal legal entities. The project aims to establish an appropriate proportion between the need to ensure a flexible mechanism for determining remuneration and bringing it as close as possible to market rules and the need for social justice. In contrast to the existing remuneration rules laid down by the chimney law proposed in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 the draft rationale for determining a different share of the variable remuneration is general in order to ensure that the demands for flexibility and reasonable remuneration are met. In addition, a very important disadvantage of this chimney law is also the lack of a system of premium effective managers, i.e. the lack of link between the management's earnings and the company's performance and the objectives achieved. The proposed law significantly changes the way in which the remuneration of company managers is determined by introducing a mechanism which forces that amount to be determined in a manner appropriate to the current situation of the company, its size and the scale of its activities. In addition, the possibility to award active managers, effectively building the company's value, is envisaged. Pursuant to the above Resolution on the rules for determining remuneration for members of the management body, they will indicate whether the total remuneration of the member of the management body will consist of a fixed fixed part constituting the basic monthly remuneration and a part of the variable supplemental remuneration for the financial year of the company. The fixed part of the monthly salary of a member of the management body will depend on the scale of the company's activities, in particular the value of its assets, revenue and employment. The result of the bill will be the professionalization of management staff and supervision in regulated entities. At the same time, the draft law contains regulations aimed at effectively countering potential abuse and pathology by introducing rules of transparency concerning the conditions for the remuneration of members of company bodies, including full information on the level of remuneration of members of management bodies. The draft law is intended to improve the efficiency of the management of companies in a short time and consequently improve their performance. The committee was held on 24 and 31 May. At the beginning of the committee meeting on 24 May, it was proposed to set up a subcommittee to consider the project. This request was not accepted by a majority of committee members. During the process of drafting the bill and the substantive discussion, MEPs proposed 31 amendments, 24 of which were accepted by the committee, most of which were legislative amendments. Let me introduce some of the most important things. Amendment in Article Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 2 (2) (4) repeals the upper employment thresholds. This amendment will facilitate the assignment to individual categories of companies with disproportionate employment to assets or turnover held by entities, which may involve the specific nature of the industry or the activities of the company. In addition, the proposed amendment will also lead to the harmonisation of the methods of categorising companies, since the current thresholds for annual turnover and the total balance sheet assets have only lower limits. This change will result in less sensitivity of the classification system to individual interpretations. Amendment in Article Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 5 makes it possible to adjust part of the variable remuneration on the boards of public companies identical to in groups of companies with the highest turnover, assets or employment. Raises the motivation element from 50 to 100%. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 It is proposed to clarify the form in which the members of the management bodies of the companies with the participation of the State Treasury will function and to define this form as a one-man business activity. In contrast, in the article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 2 (1) was proposed to narrow down the concept of fixed assets and assets of the company. These amendments to the article. 5 did not receive approval from the committee. The Commission accepted the new wording of the Article by a majority. 12 to mitigate the criteria for the inclusion of a new law other than companies in the categories of entities. Amendment introduced by Article. Paragraph 14 (10) aims to eliminate doubts as to the maximum monthly remuneration available to the self-government manager of a public health establishment following the amendment of the provision of Article. Point 8 (2) in which it is planned to increase the maximum monthly salary of persons employed in local units with legal personality from four times to six times the average monthly salary in the enterprise sector without profit payments in the 4th quarter of the previous year, announced by the President of the Central Statistical Office. At the same time, I would like to inform you that it will be corrected by the Legislative Office by mistake prescribed the title of the law in the article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6. Instead of the rules governing the remuneration of persons managing certain companies, it was written that the remuneration of persons managing certain companies was set up. The Commission asks the High House to adopt the proposed amendments and adopt the draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u244	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I am pleased to present my position on the draft law on the rules governing the remuneration of persons running certain companies. The current law on the rules of remuneration of persons who manage certain legal entities, commonly referred to as chimney law, perfectly fits the statement: theory theory and practice. It is common practice to celebrate it in the companies of the Treasury and with the majority of the State Treasury or local government units, which creates completely unjustified disparities in the earnings of members of the management bodies and supervisory boards of these companies. It is therefore appropriate to increase the impact of the ownership authorities on the level of remuneration, indicating clear and transparent criteria for determining them that do not cause the same scale of abuse as today. Therefore, we welcome the draft law submitted by the government on the rules for determining the remuneration of persons managing certain companies, which goes towards the standardization of the remuneration of managers managing companies with the participation of the State Treasury. The new rules for the remuneration of companies with the participation of the State Treasury are expected to bring nearly PLN 60 million of savings per year. The current so-called The chimney law applies only to companies in which the State Treasury has more than 50% shares. It does not cover most of the largest players, including among others. PZU, Orlen or KGHM. The provisions of the Act will be covered by companies in which the State Treasury has a share of less than 50%. The remuneration of the board members is to be 50% lower than the previous ones on average and will depend on the size of the company, the number of employees or the revenue generated. The remuneration is to be made up of a fixed part resulting from the performance of the duties and the mobile part, i.e. the bonus. In exceptional circumstances, the draft resolution on the rules for determining remuneration for members of the management body will be able to provide for a different amount of the fixed remuneration, but then the reasons for this decision will have to be published in the Public Information Bulletin, so greater transparency and transparency are assumed. The project also shows that the variable part of the remuneration in the company will not be able to exceed 50% , and in public companies 100% the basic salary of the member of the management body in the previous financial year. In addition, it is intended to depend on the investment objectives achieved by the company. These objectives are, however, mentioned in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 6 of the Act, but this calculation is not of the nature of the closed catalogue, which, while assuming the quantity and weight of these objectives separately for each company, may constitute a scope for an unjustified increase in remuneration. Awards for board members will not be discretionary. If the Supervisory Board wishes to grant bonuses to board members, it will have to indicate the investment criteria and objectives to be achieved. In addition, the various privileges of the authorities of the Treasury companies are to be abolished. It goes on, among other things. about health insurance, apartments on the house or business cars for private purposes. The draft law limits the prohibition of competition for board members after the termination of the management agreement. The amount of checks for members of the company's management board is also to be reduced to a maximum of three fixed monthly wages, subject to a minimum of one year's work in the company concerned. The draft law also regulates issues relating to the liquidation of companies and the rules on the remuneration of Liquidators. It is also in a good direction to regulate the remuneration of members of supervisory boards, which are now the same for all entities, regardless of their size. The possibility of a 10% higher remuneration will be introduced, which will only benefit the Chairman of the Supervisory Board and the members of the Audit Committee. The bill prohibits the presidents of companies of the State Treasury from receiving additional remuneration for sitting on supervisory boards of subsidiaries, i.e. in companies of daughters and grandchildren. The State Treasury Minister expressed his opinion that the implementation of this law would result in the professionalisation of the management and supervision of companies with the participation of the State Treasury. It would be good, ladies and gentlemen, if, with the entry into force of this regulation, there would also be a qualitative change in the rules of seat placement, so that managers with experience and actual competence, rather than merely formal, can assume functions on boards and supervisory boards. Such practice would certainly contribute to the professionalization of management and supervision of companies with the participation of the State Treasury. Ladies and gentlemen! I have this reflection and this idea that may be difficult to realize, but who knows. If we could create a national staff reserve, which means that managers who work, of course, operate in different companies, company boards, supervisory boards, would be catalogued or included in such a national staff reserve, they could be taken into account when filling [[♪ The bell ♪]] companies of the Treasury, so that they are not random nominations, especially party nominations. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will support this regulation, as it is very necessary and contains solutions that go in the right direction. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u284	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Rules of Procedure and Members' Affairs, I present a report on the parliamentary and draft resolutions tabled by the Bureau of the Sejm on the amendment of the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Sejm, No 333 and No 445. High Izbo! This amendment to the Rules of Procedure is a consequence of the legislative changes we have made during this term of office. Namely, we passed a new law on the Public Prosecutor's Office and it was necessary to eliminate the provisions in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm concerning the National Council of Prosecutors, to which two Members were also elected. Further legislative changes were amendments to the Act on the Ombudsman, on the Ombudsman of the rights of the child, on the protection of personal data, on the Institute of National Remembrance and on the amendment of the Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator. In general, this amendment concerns, in particular, immunity proceedings, i.e. procedures for accepting the criminal liability of persons with certain functions in state bodies, as well as the authorisation of their arrest or detention. This procedure has been regulated in these laws, which I have mentioned, and of course it must be reflected and detailed or even supplemented in the Rules of Procedure. Therefore, the designers propose to add a chapter to the Rules of Procedure. 10a, which will talk about the procedure of this procedure and, above all, the tasks in this area of the Committee on Rules of Procedure and Parliamentary Affairs, which will change its name and be called: the Committee on Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary Affairs and Immunity. It will be the task of this committee to prepare a report and, earlier at the committee's meeting, to hear the person concerned by the request for waiver of immunity, to allow it to provide explanations, to submit evidence requests, to enable it also to establish a representative who will be able to take part in the proceedings. The Commission will prepare a report to be presented to the Sejm. At the meeting of the Sejm, the person concerned will be able to take the floor and the rapporteur will be able to take the floor, while, as previously in the cases of immunity concerning Members, no discussion on this report is foreseen. High Izbo! These are the most important changes. On behalf of the committee, please accept the report. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u288	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the report of the Rules of Procedure and Parliamentary Affairs Committee and draft resolutions submitted by the Sejm Bureau on the amendment of the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Parliament, No 333 and No 445. The draft resolution submitted by the Bureau of the Sejm in print No 445 adapts the rules of procedure to the amendment of the Law on the Prosecutor's Office and the Act on the Ombudsman and other laws. The Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office was repealed by the National Public Prosecutor's Council and the rules concerning the procedures for the election of the members of the National Public Prosecutor's Office in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm have become redundant and these provisions are therefore repealed by this proposal. The amendment of the Law on the Ombudsman and some other laws is really about the amendment of six laws: on the Ombudsman, on the Supreme Chamber of Control, on the implementation of the mandate of a Member and a Senator, on the protection of personal data, on the Institute of National Remembrance and on the Ombudsman of Children's Rights, and regulates the procedure for the Sejm's consent to criminal liability, liability for offences, as well as the arrest or arrest of persons taking positions: the Ombudsman, the President of the Supreme Chamber of Control, the General Data Protection Officer, the President of the Institute of National Remembrance and the Ombudsman of Children's Rights. The Bureau of the Sejm, as an applicant for a resolution on amendments to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, proposes that proposals on this matter be dealt with by the Rules of Procedure and by the Members' Affairs, as is the case in the event of the waiver of parliamentary immunity. Consequently, in view of the change in the scope of the committee's work, this committee also changes its name to the following: the Committee on Rules of Procedure, Members' Affairs and Immunities. On the other hand, the parliamentary draft resolution from print No 333 amends the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm as regards a new way of appointing and dismissing the President of the IPN and the members of the College of National Remembrance in accordance with the initial submission of an amendment to the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance, which directly and openly politicised this institution. After the first reading, however, applicants introduced the contest procedure when they elected the President of the IPN, but in fact only as a formal screen, because in the current political situation it doesn't change anything in terms of politicalisation of IPN, but only a little bit of camouflage. However, the old method of appointment of members of the college of the institute was left as a purely political mode, without pre-selection by historical and legal environments. However, as a result of this amendment, most of the draft resolution has become obsolete, so the Rules of Procedure and Parliamentary Affairs Commission had a simplified task here, since it was practically necessary to change in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm the name of the Council of the Institute. 26 governing the selection of candidates by the College of IPN, by the assembly of electors derived from the best scientific and research units, historical in our country. Article 1 shall also be repealed. 30a on the opinion of the relevant Sejm committees of the members of the college of the Institute and in Article. 31 The paragraph abolishing the absolute majority in the election of the President of the IPN and the members of the college of the Institute shall be added and shall be amended to the ordinary majority. Although these two solutions are aggravating the democratic system in our country due to the fact that the absolute majority of votes gives a higher standard, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will support the resolution submitted according to the submission of the Rules of Procedure and Parliamentary Affairs, treating it as merely a technical solution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u290	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The provisions of the draft resolution amending the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Sejm take into account organisational changes made at the Institute of National Remembrance, a new way of appointing and dismissing the president of the Institute and members of the College of National Remembrance. The proposed changes result from the content of the Act amending the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance - Commission for the Investigation of Crimes against the Polish Nationals and on the amendment of certain other laws. Art. 15th Act on the Institute of National Remembrance provides that the Sejm of the Republic of Poland will appoint five of the nine members of the College of the Institute of National Remembrance. It is therefore necessary to regulate in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm the procedure for the appointment of persons to the College of IPN. A proposal to appoint members of the College elected by the Sejm may be submitted by the Marshal of the Sejm or by at least 35 Members. The amendment of the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the Prosecution of Crimes against the Polish Nationals and on the amendment of some other laws also provides for a new procedure for the election of the President of the IPN. According to him, the President appoints the Sejm of the Republic of Poland with the consent of the Senate, after consulting the College of the Institute of National Remembrance. It is also necessary to regulate the procedure for the election of the President in this case. The appointment of the President of the Institute, as in the case of the members of the College, will take place by a majority, which raises our doubts as to whether it would be better to make an absolute choice by a majority. The draft resolution on amending the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Parliament adapts it to the Law on the Prosecutor's Office and the Act on the Ombudsman, which has been amended. The draft Rules of Procedure provide for the adoption of a rule on the procedure for the election of Members of the National Public Prosecutor's Council, which has been abolished by the amendment of the Law on the Prosecutor's Office. The next purpose of the draft Rules of Procedure of the Sejm is to supplement the provisions governing the procedure for examining an application for consent to be held liable for a crime or offence or to arrest or arrest persons acting as ombudsman, the President of the Supreme Chamber of Control, the Ombudsman of the Child, the General Data Protection Officer and the President of the Institute of National Remembrance so that the proposal, like the proposal for the waiver of parliamentary immunity, is dealt with by the Rules of Procedure and Parliamentary Affairs, which significantly extends the scope of the committee's jurisdiction and amends its name. The amendment to the Regulation also contains minor language changes which will not significantly affect the standards contained therein. The Kukiz Club15 supports the draft amendment to the Sejm's Rules of Procedure and will vote in favour. Thank you, Marshal. [[Applause]]

It was then identified with a person supervising the production of goods, and in the 17th century mainly with a person taking risks, achieving profit or suffering loss from government contracts concerning the production of goods or the provision of services. A large proportion of companies are socially responsible companies, taking into account the interests of society without legal or administrative coercion, protecting the environment, taking into account the needs of different groups in their activities. The data from the report "Entrepreneur Image," which was prepared by the Civil Development Forum, shows that according to the resolution the day of the entrepreneur can contribute to the growth of public awareness, but will not solve the key problems of this group. This report devotes a great deal of space to young people, their knowledge of the importance of entrepreneurship for the economy and their opinions about entrepreneurs themselves. According to the report, only 53% Poles aged 15 and 19 are aware that nearly half of Polish GDP produces almost 2 million Polish companies, which in 98% are small and medium-sized enterprises. This ratio for the entire population is 72%. Such large disparities may suggest that entrepreneurship education is required, which is currently being carried out in upper secondary schools in the framework of entrepreneurial base classes. Young people raised in a market economy do not have a different image of the entrepreneur than the older generation, and opinions about private entrepreneurs depend also on the sector in which the respondents work. The less knowledge of entrepreneurs and the functioning of their companies, the more critical assessments and revealing the negative stereotype of this social group. The main problem is very low legal certainty, which results in the mediocre quality of the Polish tax system. Basic tax laws change in Poland on average every week, i.e. every week Polish businessman operates in a different tax environment. This already seems unusual, but the number of changes made in other tax laws should be added to this. And so on. Act on the rules of registration and identification of taxable persons was amended seven times in 2015, Act on flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by natural persons - six times, Act on civil law - three times. Entrepreneur's Day is important, but other fundamental issues will not be dealt with. It is necessary to ensure adequate conditions for investment, such as simple law and political stability. It must also be noted that interest in the Polish market among foreign companies is growing. The advantage of Poland is certainly its size and diversity of population. Decentralisation is also an asset. This is important when it comes to the investment process because each region is an independent entity that is attractive to investors. But the most important competitive advantage of our country is its human capital. The interest of investors in our country in the coming years will also be great. However, it is necessary to ensure stable business conditions and reduce political tensions. The establishment of 21 June Entrepreneur's Day will be an opportunity to strengthen public awareness and public knowledge of economic processes and entrepreneurship. However, let us not forget that Poland is still catching up with Europe in terms of its legal and institutional environment. The pity is all the more that Poles are genuinely one of the most entrepreneurial European nations, as evidenced by both the data and any possible research that has been carried out on this subject. This means nothing less, but that there is a huge untapped potential in our economy with bureaucratic constraints, an opaque tax system and a poor quality law. One of the biggest challenges and objectives for the coming years must therefore be the consistent conduct of a pro-freedom policy, the reduction of a large part of unnecessary regulation and the decisive liberalisation of the economy combined with prudent tax legislation, based on much higher legislative standards than currently.
