ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u342	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present today to the High House a bill on the activation of the shipbuilding and complementary industries. In line with the work schedule, the Act on Activation of the Marine Industry and Complementive Industries should be adopted by Parliament and signed by the President by the end of August 2016, so that it can enter into force in January, 1 January 2017. This is essential for the recovery of the maritime economy, especially as regards the shipbuilding industry. There is no future of maritime economy, there is no future of the Polish Coast without well-functioning shipyards, without a well-functioning shipbuilding industry. [[That's right.]] Last years, fatal last years, which have caused a lot of damage and de facto collapse of shipbuilding production, they tell us - and this was our main assumption - to take action to rebuild this industry. As in the electoral campaign, we promised and assured that within 3 months a law on the activation of the shipbuilding industry would be in place, so today I am pleased to present this document to you, after having already presented the public. The problems faced by the Pomerania, the uneven development of the Western Pomerania and the Eastern Pomerania, the far-reaching economic, social and intellectual effects of the Pomerania have just ordered us to take very rapid, directional measures to rebuild the former state. The law is a sine qua non condition, without it there is no way to rebuild what was destroyed. I would like to remind you that we currently have a 20-fold decline in shipbuilding and no ship was ordered in Szczecin in 2013. In the case of shipyards, which were some of the modern shipyards, which are important in international rankings, in these rankings we have been completely decommissioned, we are not functioning. Someone else has taken our place, someone else is competing with us today when it comes to the order market. And I'm not speaking here, of course. only about the Far East shipyards, which are governed by their laws. I am talking about a European market that has the basis and the ability to function and from which we have disappeared completely through previous decisions. This also has a huge social impact on unemployment and the unprecedented scale of emigration, economic emigration as a result of the collapse of the yard. Several dozens of thousands of people had to look for jobs, and it was people with the highest qualifications, people who should feed our industry, in Western shipyards and not Polish shipyards. This wasn't where the industries were supposed to grow, this wasn't where the intellectual continuity was supposed to be. This is the worst thing that could have happened to the economy: loss of competence, loss of importance in international markets. Of course, this has an additional aspect, even worse. This is the loss of intellectual continuity. As a result of such a policy or a policy, the technical thought was primarily affected by the greatest negative impact. It is above all the construction offices that have disappeared, it is professionals, engineers, designers who have lost their ability to build ships for our sake, today they find themselves perfectly, but unfortunately not in Poland. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft of our law focuses primarily on financial elements connected with facilitating the operation and return of Polish shipyards to competition. Of course, as I said, this is a basic condition, but not sufficient. All financial potential needs to be restored and the entire procurement market, which is the basis for credibility and then continuity of operation for the ship's industry. This also involves a huge back-up, which ceased to function, in the form of a market of suppliers, which disappeared from the Polish economy and which was extremely important, because one job in the yard gives nearly 7 jobs in the industry around the corner. Our goal, through the reconstruction of the shipbuilding industry, is to create a sub-contractor market, i.e. reindustrialisation of Poland on the basis of the reconstruction of the shipbuilding industry, and to create the economic foundations of the industry for the emergence, to rebuild precisely these competences in the area of the supplier market. As far as the details of our submission are concerned, I will discuss the most important elements of this bill. The first element is the possibility to choose between CIT/PIT - depending on the form of business carried out - and the flat-rate tax on the value of marketed production of 1% for the construction and conversion of ships under strict conditions, on the basis of a decision of the European Commission to comply with the internal market. According to our proposal, the possibility of choosing a new form of taxation will only exist during the period of the European Commission's positive decision, and therefore the Commission's work will not affect the timetable for the work on the law. The choice of this form of taxation will be made by making an appropriate statement by the trader. If this declaration is made, it will be possible to change the form of taxation no earlier than 3 years after it has been submitted. The second element is VAT of 0% for the production, import, parts and equipment of a widely defined vessel catalog. This concerns the extension of the existing catalogue in accordance with Directive 2006/112/EC on the common system of value added tax and implementing acts. The extension of tax preferences will apply to types of vessels such as sea-going tugs and sea-going icebreakers, and will cover all deliveries. By extending the catalogue of vessels subject to a preferential VAT rate, it is possible to produce these vessels in a more competitive way and with a lower financial commitment to cover the cost of materials for their production. The third element is the introduction of a mechanism to cover the special economic zone of land for shipbuilding or complementary industries after obtaining a decision by the European Commission on the compatibility of State aid with European Union law. The purpose of the amendment to the Act on Special Economic Zones is in particular to allow for the inclusion of a special economic zone of land owned by entities other than the Treasury, a single-member company of the Treasury, local government units or a communal union or in perpetual use of the manager or a single-member company of the Treasury if the investment will result in shipbuilding or complementary industries, and to add the possibility to increase the area at the request of the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs, submitted at the request of the Minister responsible for Maritime Affairs, where this is intended to develop shipbuilding or complementary industries. The last element is to allow maritime operators to benefit from European Union funds, i.e. technological credit and technological bonus, following a decision by the European Commission on the compatibility of State aid with European Union law. The amendments to the Act on certain forms of support for innovative activities result from the need to allow the shipbuilding industry to benefit from regional aid under the loan for innovative technologies. At present, the technological bonus cannot be granted for shipbuilding activities. The aim of the amendment is to enable Polish entrepreneurs to benefit from the powers deriving from the guidelines on regional aid for the years 2014 to 2020 which allow, after notification to the European Commission, the possibility of supporting the shipbuilding sector. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'm handing over the bill for further work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u382	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! When presenting a draft law on amending the law on value added tax and on amending certain other laws, I would first like to mention the reasons that led to the introduction of the proposed solutions becoming necessary and, subsequently, to indicate the most important changes which the proposal will introduce into the legal order. For more than 5 years, the Polish liquid fuel market has experienced a grey zone and tax fraud, whose form and scale have gone far beyond the level of naturally occurring market economy. During these few years, irregularities in the circulation of liquid fuels have reached a size that is a real threat to the sustainability of the fuel industry and thus have a significant impact on the level of budgetary revenue. Fuel companies, both smaller and large, are forced to compete with tax criminals and, unfortunately, often face a choice: either to eliminate their activities or to cooperate with criminals, putting themselves at risk of criminal and tax consequences. For fuel companies, it is a matter of decision to withdraw from the Polish market or to look for a sale for fuel abroad. And today's fuel criminals are not small groups of smugglers or owners of small manufactures, but they are well organized and oriented in the legal regulations and fuel logistics of an international criminal group. Therefore, the loss of the state budget is not counted in millions, but in billions of zlotys. Further losses to the economy due to the direct impact of the shadow economy on honest entrepreneurs cannot be counted. The grey fuel trading zone covers a number of issues, not just tax issues, but a model of large-scale fraud, which emerged in 2011, is particularly dangerous and requires decisive intervention in the first place. It is based on the introduction into the country of fuels formally from other Member States of the European Union, but mostly from outside the Union. The cheater uses the applicable VAT accounting rules for intra-Community purchases of fuels when the introduction of fuel into the territory of the country does not result in the payment of VAT, whereas the time when VAT is declared and paid for the sale of that fuel is due only when the entity has already disappeared from the market. Thus, the tax offence occurs only on expiry of the tax deadline, when the tax services most often have no one left to look for. The second element conducive to fraud is the fact that fuel is an excise product and there are two entities in intra-Community acquisitions, each being a taxable person of a different tax. The legal and economic purchaser of the fuel is only a VAT taxable person, while the tax warehouse or registered consignee, i.e. the entities established for the purpose of supervising the movement of excise goods, are excise taxable persons, while from a legal, economic and tax point of view they are not the buyers of the fuel, the parties to the transaction or the taxable persons of the VAT. If it is added that the supervision of each of these entities is exercised by a different authority, by the VAT tax office and by the customs office of excise duty, then comprehensive supervision of the shipment of fuels to the country is not effective. The third element of fraud is the relatively high ease of obtaining allowances for the trading of fuel from abroad in the form of concessions. However, the licensing of fuel trading is not organised in such a way that there is a sufficient link between the existence of a concession and the availability of an adequate infrastructure enabling it to operate in this respect. As a result, there are operators who can operate on a large scale in the country without any infrastructure, and it should be borne in mind that the trading of fuels with foreign fuels involves a very large number of obligations, among others. as regards the maintenance of mandatory stocks or the achievement of the national indicative objective. The initiatives taken so far to reduce the grey economy in the fuel market can be considered to be far insufficient and be characterised as the easiest attempt to hinder criminal activity, which, after a temporary action effect, further improves the mechanisms of fraud. It should be pointed out that, despite these trials, the scale of tax irregularities identified by the tax control doubles with each year. While in 2012 these irregularities were estimated at about PLN 500 million, in 2015 it was almost PLN 6 billion. The figures also show the impact of the involvement of state authorities, which conduct thousands of checks annually to detect tax frauds, but despite improving the methodology of the Ministry of Finance services in close cooperation with law enforcement authorities and their counterparts abroad, the effect is not satisfactory. The fight against VAT carousels is one of the most difficult tasks of the control authorities, and it involves considerable resources, but under current legal conditions the execution of this task is only doomed to a more or less effective minimization of the losses of the state budget. As a result, we are in a situation where every day the Polish border completely exceeds several hundred tanks, carrying billions of litres of fuel that hit our market each year. Each litre is approx. 1 PLN of unpaid VAT, not to mention the unfulfilled obligation of the indicative target and the unreplaced compulsory stocks. The Internet is full of offers to sell any quantity of imported fuel with direct transport in any place in the country at a price which, for the above reasons, is not able to offer any other legal entity in the country. Legislative changes are therefore needed. The project prepared by the government was called a working fuel package. It is the result of the answer to the question: How can we effectively combat the grey fuel market? What factors determine that the grey zone can function? Previously, I have identified three elements characterizing tax fraud in the trading of fuels, and it is precisely the joint change of these elements that is a proposal that will systematically prevent this model from working. The proposed changes are closely linked and complement each other. The basic amendment including the amendment to the VAT Act is to approximate the rules on taxation of intra-Community purchases of fuels to those applying to domestic transactions. The project introduces an obligation to pay VAT at the time of the introduction of fuel into the territory of the country, while today the obligation to pay this tax arises only after the resale of the fuel. At the same time, the principle of accounting for the tax on declarations will not change and the tax paid when the fuel is introduced into the country will reduce the tax due for the sale of fuel to domestic customers. The guarantee of the correct circulation of VAT for the introduction of fuel into the country is the establishment of a taxable person for tax warehouses and registered customers who provide a service for the import of fuel under a suspended excise procedure. Thus, tax warehouses and registered customers who, as taxable persons of excise duty, service the introduction of fuel into their territory, will be obliged to collect from their customers, i.e. fuel owners, VAT and to pay it to the same body in which they pay excise duty. The second amendment proposed in the Excise Tax Act concerns the mere provision of fuel to other entities by tax warehouses and registered customers in the territory of the country. The basic condition for the use of the services of tax warehouse and registered customer will be that the entity purchasing fuel in another European Union country holds a concession for the trading of liquid fuels abroad. In addition to the concession, this entity will have to have its registered office or branch in our country and register for VAT purposes. This change will prevent more and more widespread situations of fuel supply chains, where another entity formally purchases fuel to the country using the services of a registered customer and another is obliged to settle VAT for this purpose. The introduction of the principle that in any case the entity purchasing the fuel into the country is a licensed trader and VAT taxable person will make it impossible to avoid payment of VAT. First, such a tax will be required to collect and pay the tax warehouse or registered consignee, and secondly, the tax licence of the OPZ will constitute a property security of up to 10 million as payment of that tax. The third element is the change in the fuel trading licensing system with abroad, i.e. amendments to the Energy Law Act. Given the sensitivity of international trade to fraud, it is appropriate to introduce greater credibility requirements and tools for the President of energy regulation to be more effective in overseeing those involved. Greater requirements are the introduction of a statutory ban on the representation of fuel operators by punished persons and the extension of the transparency of licence applicants. In particular, it is essential for the efficiency of the oversight of foreign fuel trading entities to require that applicants have a seat or a branch in the territory of the country and registration for VAT purposes. This change will allow for real supervision of all obligations relating to the introduction of fuels into the country, both tax and energy security of the country, i.e. mandatory stocks, and related to environmental protection, i.e. ensuring the use of biofuels at the level determined by the State. The latest change is the link between the licensing process for international fuel trading and the registration process and the maintenance of mandatory fuel stocks. The amendments introduced by the Energy Law and the Wrestling Act set out the process of obtaining allowances for the fully legitimate production and removal of fuels from abroad. This means that an entity seeking a concession will be required to demonstrate to the President of the Energy Regulation that it has started the process of guaranteeing the maintenance of mandatory stocks. The concession will allow him to apply to the President of the Material Reserve Agency for entry in the register of entities holding mandatory stocks. The project presented is a comprehensive solution which will effectively address the biggest problems facing the Polish liquid fuel market today and which brings multi-billion-dollar losses to the Polish state. Therefore, on behalf of the Government of the Republic of Poland, I ask the High House to support this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u394	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour to present my position on the government's draft Print Act No. 554. The bill deals with changes in the rules on licensing of foreign liquid fuels, the system for collecting VAT on the acquisition of intra-Community fuels and the conditions under which liquid fuels are imported into the country under suspension of excise duty. Currently, the intra-Community acquisition of liquid fuels does not result in the payment of VAT, and the tax is only subject to its resale in Poland, which simply creates an opportunity for abuse. After the changes, the obligation to pay the tax will generally arise within 5 days of the introduction of the fuel into the country. The tax thus paid will reduce the tax due for payment resulting from the tax return, which is obvious. If intra-Community liquid fuels are purchased using the tax warehouse or registered customer, they will be taxable persons for VAT. The project also provides for amendments to the Energy Law Act, among others. by increasing licensing requirements as well as increasing the powers of the President of the Energy Regulatory Office to examine the reliability of licence applicants. The project assumes that concessions for the trading of fuels with foreign countries will only be granted to entities established in Poland or foreign companies that have registered a branch in our country and will introduce liquid fuels into Poland through it. This will link the obligation to license the trading of liquid fuels with the status of VAT taxable person and to increase the efficiency of state supervision. The draft also amends the excise tax law, preventing the circumvention of the protective function of concessions for the trading of fuels with abroad. It is proposed to limit the performance of the service function by tax warehouses and registered customers. In practice, it is intended to consist of the possibility of providing services exclusively to entities established in Poland or foreign entities having a branch established in our country. This will only apply to the purchase of motor fuels and does not cover excise duty-free fuels. These new solutions envisaged for the project are due to apply from 1 August this year. In summary, the proposed project is another project which aims to eliminate tax fraud, in particular to close the VAT gap. In the opinion of the government, the sealing resulting from this project is the amounts expressed in billions of zlotys. And as for the purpose itself or the need of this law, the Polish People's Party has no objections. However, our doubts and attention are aroused by the way we work on the project. I would like to point out here that the government is once again returning to a bad way of proceding and establishing the law, we have already used to call it in our parliament the creation of law on the knee, which, of course, means the quality of such a law. This government project was not even subject to inter-ministerial agreements, it does not have any analyses or opinions from legislative and supervisory institutions, as well as business representatives. The government seems to forget that it creates a law not only for fraudsters, but for all entrepreneurs, also for honest ones, and for them a restrictive law is not needed. This is why consultation seems to be really useful to a large extent. My club, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, will, of course, be in favour of transferring the project to further work. Thank you.

I am unfortunately forced to note that the bill submitted contains a fundamental logical error. Diet is not a remuneration and in the light of many regulations existing in the Polish legal system cannot be regarded as such. Parliamentary diet is in fact an equalisation of the costs incurred by Members and Senators in the exercise of their mandate in the country. In a similar way, the diets of other persons performing activities related to social and civic duties are treated similarly, e.g. Councillors' allowances at the various levels of local government are defined as compensation for lost earnings and reimbursement of the costs associated with the fulfilment of the mandate. Therefore, a parliamentary diet cannot be treated on an equal footing with remuneration, especially since it is also entitled to the so-called. Members who are not members of Parliament. The wording of the article also raises doubts. 3 of the proposal assuming that the provisions of Article. 1 and 2 shall apply to revenue received from 1 January 2016. Changes during the tax year shall undermine the prohibition of retroactivity. In addition, in its judgment of 18 November 2014, e.g. Act K 23/12, the Constitutional Tribunal indicated that amendments to the rules on personal income tax should not be amended in the course of the tax year with regard to the subject matter, entity, tax base and rates of that tax. These criteria are without doubt directly affected by the amount of the tax-free amount. The explanatory memorandum of the Act stresses that citizens are equal to the law and should have equal tax-free amounts. I agree. However, if, with full consequence, this argument is accepted in the context of the request for taxation of the parliamentary diet, then the diet of local, district and voivodeship council should also be taxed. Therefore, the argument concerning the need to bring about the tax treatment of a parliamentary diet in order to align the tax-free amount concerned with other tax-free amounts, is about other citizens of the Republic of Poland, is nothing more than cheap populism, as the applicant himself has just admitted. It should also be noted, ladies and gentlemen, that this problem also applies to a whole range of workers who benefit from their work travel allowances. This matter must therefore be treated uniformly, not selectively, only towards parliamentarians. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle are, of course, in favour of raising the tax-free amount, but telling citizens that Members have a higher tax-free amount is not true. Citizens will say, what's his name, but Members are the bad guys who feed at the expense of other citizens. Excuse me, but this is going to build further divisions in already conflicted Polish society. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u470	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the Government's draft law on anti-terrorist activities and on the amendment of some other laws, print No 516. The draft law presented by the Council of Ministers is the implementation of the principle: zero tolerance for terrorists. The draft law contains a number of appropriate tools for state authorities to deal with terrorist threats. The state has a duty to guarantee us Poles a sense of security. It is worth recalling that there is no sense of security, no sense of freedom without a real sense of security. This is an issue that affects all of us, regardless of political views and election preferences. This is our business together. High Izbo! Two important events ahead of us, which are a huge opportunity to consolidate and strengthen the image of Poland on the international stage. Already in a month, in July, the NATO summit will take place in Warsaw, and 2 weeks later the World Youth Days will begin and the visit of Pope Francis to the Republic of Poland will begin. As many as 2 million people from 185 countries around the world can participate in World Youth Day. This means doing everything we can to ensure that state institutions such as the Police, the Border Guard, the Fire Department, the Special Forces and the Armed Forces can have real tools to ensure security for all participants in these events. Let us remember that the democratic rule of law, which is the Republic of Poland, is a guarantee of freedom and civil rights, while the real threat to the freedom of citizens are terrorists, ruthless, unpredictable and ready for everything. The purpose of the draft law on anti-terrorist activities are among others.: Better coordination of service work, comprehensive regulation of identification, counter and counter terrorism in a single legal act, increased protection of ICT systems of public administrations, introduction of four levels of alarm in accordance with NATO terminology, increase the link for perpetrators of false alarms up to at least 10 000. zł. During the drafting of the bill, the Administrative and Home Affairs Commission introduced a number of amendments at its meetings on 20 May and 7 June 2016. I would like to present to the High House a few amendments to the bill. Firstly, there is a restriction on the activities involved, including operational and exploratory activities coordinated by the Head of the Internal Security Agency, to terrorist incidents. Changes have been made regarding the possibility of taking biological material to determine DNA from a person who is not a citizen of the Republic of Poland. ABW, Police and Border Guard officers are entitled to download images of fingerprints or to capture facial images, or non-invasive collection of biological material to determine the DNA profile of a person who is not a citizen of the Republic of Poland, where there is doubt about the identity of a person, there is a suspicion of an illegal crossing of the Polish border or of the declared purpose of staying in the territory of the Republic of Poland, there is a suspicion of an illegal presence in the territory of Poland, there is a suspicion of a relationship of a person with a terrorist event or a person who could participate in terrorist training. It is further specified that the alerting steps shall be invoked immediately after the risk or the consequences of an event which is a prerequisite for their introduction have been minimised. The scope of the special use of weapons has been extended in counter-terrorist action to include the situation of the release of the hostage. The possibility of helping police units of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland was introduced not only after the introduction of the third or fourth level of alarm, but also for example. in preparation for planned mass events, even when no alert level has been introduced. The penalty for participating in terrorist training has been increased, imprisonment has been imposed from 3 months to 5 years for a person who participates in training to enable such a crime to be committed for the purpose of committing a terrorist offence. The deadline until 1 February 2017 was extended to which prepaid service subscribers are obliged to provide their prepaid service provider data. It was also introduced that the Minister responsible for Home Affairs of the Marshals of the Sejm and Senate of the Republic of Poland should immediately inform about the introduction of the third or fourth level of the alert, and the Marshals will forward this information to the Members and Senators. Vacatio legis has been introduced, the law shall enter into force 7 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. 43, which shall enter into force 30 days after the date of the announcement. There have also been obvious editorial amendments aimed at clarifying, harmonising and increasing transparency in the provisions of the draft law. The amendments to the bill adopted by the committee were tabled by Members from parliamentary clubs Law and Justice, Civic Platform, Kukiz,15 and Modern. This is proof of the possibility of treating security in the Polish Parliament as a priority issue, which should be excluded from current political disputes. It's too serious to be hysterical about attacking good solutions that can help save human life. More than 60 amendments were tabled in the course of the draft law in committee. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs recommends that the High House adopt a draft law and reject 26 minority proposals submitted during parliamentary work. Let us give a clear signal that there is a zero tolerance rule for terrorists in Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[No tolerance for the opposition, sir.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u482	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Poselski Club15 on the Anti-Terrorism Act. This is the second reading of this law, an undoubtedly necessary act, because it equips the State with legal instruments necessary to take adequate measures to respond to terrorist incidents in Poland. The development and adoption of cooperation mechanisms for all services, both civilian and military, bodies and institutions, local authorities and the private sector, is necessary to achieve this objective. There are many elements in this law, which must be clearly assessed as good, necessary and even necessary. These include issues such as the regulation of statutory emergency degrees, the determination of procedures for the use of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, in particular special forces, counter-terrorism activities, the possibility of effective operation of uniformed services in hostage situations or the possibility of carrying out anti-terrorist and rescue activities at sea in the Polish area of responsibility of the SAR. At the same time, it should be noted that the act is being processed at a rapid pace. The timing of two major events, which will take place soon in Poland, which is of course the summit of NATO and World Youth Days, can be justified. However, motivated by the need to proceed swiftly with the withdrawal from the public hearing requested by Mr Kukiz15, a negative assessment must be made. Why was it so important? Because this law also involves restricting civil liberties, which arouses great emotions in society. While some of the provisions of this law, which increase the security of citizens at the expense of giving up part of citizens' freedoms, must be regarded as legitimate, some of them raise huge controversy with doubts about their usefulness in combating terrorist threats. This applies primarily to the attempt to introduce the need to register all prepaid cards. We believe that these records are unnecessary, harmful and completely unnecessary. They will in no way contribute to a better fight against terrorism. We consider the resistance and social opposition that these provisions raise to be fully justified, and therefore we request that this provision be removed from the draft law. The weakness of this project is also the lack of acceptance of the definition of terrorism, although it is a strict act dedicated to combating this type of action. As a definition, it is impossible to recognise the existing regulations adopted in Polish criminal law, according to which what is considered to be a terrorist offence and what is not, determines the amount of the penalty and the purpose of the action. The nature of terrorism, i.e. use or threat of use of force or violence, or motives of the perpetrator, although crucial and defined e.g. the terms and definitions of NATO have not been taken into account in the vocabulary. There is therefore no distinction between ideologically motivated, politically or religiously motivated terrorism and criminal terrorism, which is common and most often robbery. The very dangerous consequences are also due to the provision introduced by the Act which, in the event of the need for counter-terrorist action, provides for the remaining troops and troops to assist the police in the command system of the Armed Forces. Why are we judging him negatively? Because the effective conduct of counter-terrorist actions requires uniformity of the command system for the operation at the place of its operation. Soldiers who will assist the police on the spot to eliminate the terrorist threat should therefore be subject to the person coordinating the action. Effective command requires decision making, sometimes within seconds, which can determine the lives of many people. That is why we have tabled an amendment on this issue and we hope that it will meet with the support of the parliamentary majority. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This law is undoubtedly necessary, and Kukiz15 will therefore support it once the relevant amendments have been made. At the same time, we would also like to join some of them. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u603	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present to the High House a draft law amending the Law on the Education System and some other laws. The present draft amendment of the Law of 7 September 1991, hereinafter referred to as the Act, proposes a number of amendments. The two changes are systemic in nature, the other changes are largely clearer and new solutions proposed for a long time by different educational environments. The main systemic change concerns the abolition of the final year of primary education from the 2016/2017 school year. At present, entry to this test is a condition for completion of primary school and the results of the check are not taken into account when receiving pupils to secondary school. The results of the test are taken into account when recruiting for bilingual schools, international branches and sports classes. As a result, recruitment to such schools and junior high schools, which carry out such classes, was also subject to a recruitment change. The managing authorities will be able to establish recruitment rules. In the Act we propose that three subjects - Polish language, mathematics and modern foreign language - should be taken into account in this recruitment. With regard to the level of knowledge and skills of pupils who were tested after primary school, we propose for these schools and for those pedagogical councils wishing to conduct an educational study of added value to carry out this diagnostic test on a voluntary basis in the first grade of junior high school. This diagnostic test will be prepared by the Central Examination Board. In electronic form, he will reach schools. Those schools that wish to carry out this test on their own will organise the test and evaluate the tests already completed by the students. The second important issue concerns the appointment of the College of Examination Arbitration to deal with appeals against the decisions of the district directors of the examination committees. After this first stage, which is already in the current educational law, a senior has the right to look into his prom thesis and may apply to the district director of the examination commission for a review of the points if he considers that this score has been wrongly completed. In the current state of law, this was the final decision of the district director of the examination commission. Depending, of course, on the situation, these results were improved in favour of the student or were not changed. At the moment, the College of Examination Arbitration, given that there have been a lot of applications over the past years, the protests of graduates, there have even been legal cases, we know that this graduation exam, the maturity exam is often also an entrance exam for studies, so students, especially in the case of subjects such as chemistry, biology, mathematics, filed appeals and fought for their right, as I mentioned, even before the courts... The College of Arbitration is therefore set up at the Director of the Central Examination Board, composed of independent experts and the evaluation of the College is a final assessment. As far as the examination is concerned, we introduce the possibility of taking photographs of the examination work during the examination. This also applies to the secondary examination and the examination confirming professional qualifications. This is certainly a great relief from the work of the district committees, because always such an insight took place in the presence of a member of the district examination commission and a graduate, the abiterator could only take notes. More shifts. We introduce a change after the change, because in the current legal state the rules of recruitment, and above all the terms of recruitment and the deadlines for submitting documents, are included in the Education System Act. This legal situation has already led to many protests, many discussions, at the drafting stage of this law. Therefore, in the recruitment proceedings for the 2017/2018 school year and for the following years, we want the time limits for this recruitment procedure supplementing the submission of documents to be fixed, as was the case before, by the guidance bodies or the competent school curator in accordance with his/her competence. We have also established recruitment rules from the 2017/2018 school year for the next years to the newly created public kindergarten, public other form of preschool education, public school or public institution. It was a legal loophole, it needed to be filled. Since 1 September 2016, we have introduced the possibility for primary and secondary school heads to be able to purchase textbooks or educational materials to learn a particular modern language according to the degree of advanced knowledge of this language. The cost of providing a manual or educational material is specified in the Act - the amount of refund up to 25 PLN per pupil. These costs will be covered by a special grant for each school year. We are introducing changes in textbooks and materials for disabled students. This is another area, the current law has basically put huge obstacles to supplying those special school pupils who received a ruling during the school year, and even in such a situation when the student lost or destroyed the manual, there was no possibility of supplementing, buying this textbook, only in the following school year, from the next grant it could be financed. Therefore, we have made changes to improve this situation in favour of the student and so that the student does not have to wait a few or several months for a textbook. We also want this grant to allow you to buy hardware or software for reading textbooks, educational materials in electronic form. An additional help in working with a student with special educational needs will be the possibility to finance the so-called grants. a book supporting the special education of disabled pupils. This will definitely contribute to improving the quality of education for these pupils. Also in the area of students with disabilities and the preparation of materials for them, we have added the possibility to include experts on manuals for training of special NGOs, NGOs - perhaps in the plural number, which carry out statutory activities in this area, including scientific and educational activities. We raise a very important issue in this law, and in fact we are taking a very big step, not yet to say that it is nice, but it is big, to prepare the Polish education system for the education of students coming from abroad. This is a huge social problem in some regions of Poland at the moment. Even close to us, near Warsaw, we have children who are not covered by school duties, the obligation to study, children of foreigners who came to Poland to work. Therefore, we clarify the catalogue of local self-government units obliged to provide free language learning to Polish students coming from abroad, because there are municipalities in which whole schools would be children of foreigners. We introduce the possibility of fulfilling the school obligation in a circuit other than the child's, or in a community other than that in which the foreigner's child lives. We clarify the mandate for the Minister of Education and Education to issue a regulation on forms and methods of education, to adapt forms and methods of education to the needs of non-Polish citizens and Polish citizens who have studied abroad. Of course, it is primarily about helping these children learn Polish so that they can assimilate with the class team as soon as possible. Another regulation concerns a very important issue. We are in such a situation at the moment that it is the inconsistent, imprecise law that has caused huge financial problems for local governments, mostly district governments. The case concerns the control of tax chambers in MOWs and MOSs, i.e. in sociotherapy centres and educational centres. There was a question of the definition of a parent. In view of the fact that this definition was not clarified and that the interpretation of the existing rules was different, there are unfortunately high reimbursements of subsidies. We want to settle this matter as soon as possible, and in this amendment we introduce the act on s.i.o. a definition that has already been adopted, which states that the pupil - a student, not a pupil - is the pupil's pupil, who has received a referral to the directorate of MOW or MOS. Next, the whole big block, a huge block on clarification of concepts, deadlines and rules relating to the calculation of grants for public and non-public schools, kindergartens and other forms of preschool education. This is another area that has been the subject of court cases and civil proceedings over the last few years. As a result, this applies to the huge amounts that local governments have in their civil proceedings from non-public entities. We have come to the conclusion that this legal situation needs to be changed as soon as possible and clarified. These systemic changes are not far-reaching. All requests that have been received by the Ministry in this regard will be examined in the context of a major revision of the Education Act, but today the need for clarification was so great that we decided that these provisions should enter into force from 1 January 2017. In addition to local government units, regional accounting chambers, the Supreme Audit Chamber and non-J.S.T. entities, which run various educational institutions, reported the need for amendment. And so we introduced into the dictionary the concept of the nearest municipality and the nearest district running a given school or institution, we defined the concept of current expenditure, which is the basis for calculating the amount of the grant, and we proposed some exemptions in this respect so that they were definitely defined in the Act. There has been a clarification and adjustment of the rules on the settlement between municipalities of grant costs or pre-school education costs where the child does not live in a given municipality but benefits from pre-school education. We have clarified the issue of subsidising children with intellectual disabilities to a deep extent. Since, under the provisions of education law, children with a profound disability are not students, we had to clarify that it was for the education and education classes for these children that a grant of the amount laid down in the subsidy is granted. In order to make the calculation of grants as transparent and transparent as possible, we propose that basic data for the calculation of grants should be made available to the public by the local government. I'd still have a few points, if I had that card, you have to say it like that, handy. There is also a block of changes that concern artistic schools, from the application of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage. And in this respect... I'm sorry, there's another page... on 1 September 2018 there's a post-library school and a cultural animator school. The Minister of Culture believes that these professions should be taught at the level of higher education. Provisions on the rules for subsidising non-public artistic schools with public school powers shall be clarified. The issues related to entry into the register of non-public schools shall also be clarified. High Izbo! As I mentioned, the amendment is quite large, but these are not entirely new things that are introduced into the system, which must be analysed and assessed from the beginning. Most of these are good changes that regulate the current legal situation at such a large interface of local government - educational entities. Thank you very much. Any questions, please. [[Applause]]

For example, in September 2015. Three-day workshops summarizing one of the stages of the EWD study, organized for several hundred university employees in one of the most expensive hotels in Warsaw, brought nothing, and probably cost about PLN 0.5 million. Several people have lived for many years from the project itself, and it has been successful. But the law on amending the law on the system of education also introduces a number of other detailed solutions concerning the functioning of education in Poland. The paradox is that in this way, sometimes through excessive bureaucracy, legislators want to ensure, and probably ensure, rational spending on education funds. The aim is to define in detail what a grant is, which should not be taken into account in determining its amount and for what it is intended to be. This shows the concern of the Ministry that educational needs will not be adequately secured by local government units without introducing almost retail ways of calculating and transferring grants to education for children. But this is probably due to the ministry's concern about the inappropriate use of the funds provided by grants. Many changes also apply to teaching pupils with different types of dysfunctions. These changes are really going in the right direction and are designed to provide conditions for their decent teaching and learning. It is necessary to take away, for example, the conditions set out in the procedated law for passing the exams to be provided to students with dysfunctions. They are not so much used to reduce the requirements relating to the passability of exams as they are intended to make it easier for pupils to pass them and to ensure that the examiners are given a fair and fair assessment. It is also useful to read the definition of conditions for subsidising and maintaining non-public educational establishments operating in special forms of activity, e.g. early support for the development of the child. Very often local self-government units avoided this or did not have a sufficient legal basis for providing financial assistance to non-public educational institutions and, when they provided it, often had administrative or legal problems. The bill removes this obstacle. The law also goes in the right direction when it comes to adapting education to the requirements of the modern labour market. That's a good idea. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u675	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources and some other laws. It's a very good, pro-development law for the RES industry. The proposed bill proposes far-reaching changes which, in my opinion, will affect the unlimited development of investments in renewable energy sources. It is extremely important to introduce a prosumer institution, which will be both a producer and a consumer of electricity. For example, a school, a clinic, a natural person, a farmer, a housing community, a religious union and many other entities that will generate energy in installations up to 40 kW for their own needs, but not for sale. I think this is the essence of prosumerism - not for sale, ladies and gentlemen. The surplus of energy will be released into the network and then collected in the annual account, under the so-called. net metering, in the size depending on the size of the installation or the public aid received. For prosumer, the energy network will be an energy storage facility from which it will be possible to collect, using a quantitative drain mechanism. The quantity void is justified by the fact, ladies and gentlemen, that there is nothing for free. Energy plants, transmission networks also bear the costs of this, and if someone does not earn, that means that the entire industry will have problems. That's why this is a rational mechanism. We have to remember that both sides of this entire market have to make money, they have to benefit from it. Prosumers will be able, like other RES market participants, to create clusters and energy cooperatives. This is of great importance because possible energy surpluses produced by individual cluster members will be able to be sold inside the cluster to other members who consume more energy than they produce. The opposite concept of RES market development was the model based on guaranteed tariffs, which in fact blocked the development of this sector of the economy. It is correct to say that the tariffs guaranteed by the RES market are concrete. The 800 MW limit in this system would mean only 100 and 120 000. installation for a handful of participants on a first-come, first-served basis. Thus, there are completely unfounded voices concerning the alleged liquidation of the RES industry as a result of this amendment. The 800 MW limit results from an assessment of the impact of the implementation of the regulation, which was introduced in February 2015. Social links, innovation, cooperation between local prosumers and local self-government units on the basis of common generation and consumption of electricity, heat and cold will lead to innovation within clusters and energy cooperatives. As part of the draft law, the Commission for Energy and Treasury adopted a number of important amendments. The proposed amendments were supported by the Consumer Federation. At its meeting of 7 June, the committee clarified the regulation on balancing interphase energy from micro-installations. This change will bring huge savings to prosumers. The regulation related to the production of energy from the so-called "energy production" needs to be clarified. contaminated biomass. This may allow abuse and extortion of additional green certificates by intentionally increasing the calorific value of the combustion mass in power plants. Regulation also requires the problem of the existing system of green certificates. Prices of green certificates in session quotations on the Freight Exchange have been steadily falling in recent weeks and have recently reached a record low: below 70 PLN per MWh. This results in problems for a large part of the industry. Together with a group of Members, I hereby propose an amendment to clarify the definition of biomass, so that the law is even more pro-developmental, encouraging innovation, giving impetus to building an energy mix that ensures energy security. The development of renewable energy sources must go hand in hand with the restructuring of Polish mining and Polish conventional energy, which are the foundation of Polish energy security. The Free and Solidarni Circle recommends that the bill be sent back to the Energy and Treasury Commission for further work. I would like to stress that I am firmly convinced that the solutions proposed in this bill are correct. I believe that this will be a far-reaching strong boost to the development of the whole renewable energy sector. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] Marshal, I submit an amendment signed by 16 Members.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u693	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to you a draft law amending the law on medicinal activities and certain other laws. The changes proposed in the project relate to the treatment of net loss, i.e. the negative financial result of the public health institution itself. They concern the prohibition on the sale of shares or shares in companies of the Treasury or local government units if they lose a majority holding or shares as a result of the sale. I would like to point out that a similar solution already exists for companies set up by medical universities. The third amendment concerns the introduction of the principle of non-payment of dividends in capital companies in which the Treasury or local government units have majority shares or shares. So this previous and next point are the assumptions of the operation of the non-profit company. The next amendment concerns the strengthening of the supervisory tools of the entity creating its SPOZ. These tools require the manager to draw up an annual report on the financial and economic situation. The report will include a forecast of the economic and financial situation for the next 3 financial years and an analysis of the economic and financial situation for the previous year. This analysis will be carried out using country-wide economic and financial indicators, which will be prepared together with the Minister of Finance of the Minister of Health. Another amendment concerns the definition of the control mode carried out by the voivode when information is taken on the disposal by a medicinal user of medicines or medical devices contrary to the prohibition under the Pharmaceutical Law. The next amendment clarifies the rules on the conduct of controls on medicinal products. Another concerns the transfer by the real estate maker to an independent public health establishment. As a reminder, the only form at the moment is to transfer it free of charge. We would like the SPOZ to be based on all available rights of ownership of these properties. Another amendment concerns the regulation of the rules of cooperation of medicinal products with the police. This applies to missing persons, so-called "missing persons." persons N.N., and minors who are not in contact with a statutory representative. The next changes relate to the terminology changes, i.e. the conversion of the term "business" into the words "medical establishment" and changes in the terminology that adapts the terms used to the Accounting Act. This is about replacing the negative financial result with a net loss. The next amendment concerns the waiver of the obligation to have medical accident insurance. I would like to remind you that the deferral of this obligation has been postponed several times over time and that this has been due to the absence of insurance companies that would be interested in such insurances. There was practically a monopoly on the market which caused the prices of these insurances to be It's practically unacceptable. Therefore, we propose that we move away from this obligation. Another amendment introduces the possibility for local self-government units to finance healthcare services. The next allows the transfer of responsibilities and powers of the entity constituting the SPOZ. This is about the possibility of handing over e.g. by the governor of his subject in rule e.g. A marshal. We allow the possibility of creating a SPOZ. We would like to introduce a number of provisions that simplify the pursuit of medicinal activities. There is also a change in the optional statutory authorisation for the Minister of Health to issue standards for medical procedures. Finally, we are introducing special regulations for medicinal entities that operate in the form of budgetary units for which the constituent bodies are: the Minister of Justice, the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, the Head of the ABW, the Head of the Intelligence Agency or the Head of the Central Anti-corruption Office. Dear Marshal! Dear Members, I ask you kindly to adopt a law in the form proposed by the government. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u695	Dear Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position regarding a government draft law amending the Law on Medical Activity and some other laws. The project aims to improve the activities of the SPOZ and, as a consequence, to increase the accessibility of citizens to medical services. The proposed changes are systemic, rather serious, and relate to a wide legal environment for medicinal activities in many aspects. The proposed changes concern inter alia. - here I will have to partially repeat the Minister's argument, I have chosen only the most important points due to the limited time - the proceedings in the event of a net loss, i.e. a negative result of the financed SPOZ. In the event of a net loss, the SPOZ is to cover it on its own, i.e. by reducing the bet fund. He's got nine months. If the bet fund is not sufficient, the creation entity will be obliged to cover the resulting part of the loss, but not more than the amount resulting from the net loss and depreciation costs. However, if SPOZ was unable to cover the net losses on its own, it would be possible to close it. We then propose to prohibit private parties from selling shares or shares in capital companies involving the State Treasury or local government units if they are to lose control of medicinal products. The aim is, as it is not hard to guess, to maintain the influence of the State on the functioning of medicinal products, so that every citizen can be guaranteed a constitutional right to health protection. Thirdly, we propose to prohibit the payment of dividends in capital companies in which the Treasury or local government units will have a share of more than 51% shares or shares. The aim and profits are to be transferred to the healthcare provider, to investments and to wage increases. Then we introduce the possibility of buying health care benefits by the local government, but with entities for which it is the body for which it is the creator. I stress that this is a change that will probably arouse a lot of emotions, that we are introducing an opportunity, not an obligation. Another important change - for the head of the SPOZ we impose an obligation to draw up a report on the economic and financial situation of the SPOZ in the previous year and, for 3 financial years forward, the forecasts of the economic and financial situation to be submitted by 3 May each year to the lead authority. Objective - to increase the supervision of the unit supervisor. It is also important to give up the necessity of compulsory medical accident insurance. As the Minister said, it has been tried on for a very long time, these insurances were very expensive, heavily charged the hospital's budget. Then we propose changes to facilitate the exercise of medicinal activities, namely we exempt the heads of medicinal entities from the obligation to provide the State Sanitary Inspection and the local authorities with information on the number of persons who pursue medical profession under civil law contracts and as a professional practice. The procedure for registration of entities engaged in medical activities has also been simplified. We have proposed that the head of the healthcare provider conducts a competition for the post of his deputy - so far such a competition has been conducted by the founding body, which significantly prolongs the procedure. We have also replaced certain terms with others, that is to say, we do not specify the company, we introduce a more appropriate term, I think, the term "medical bet," and we also do not define the negative financial result, we introduce the term "net loss" in accordance with the Accounting Act. I would like to reiterate once again that the essence of the proposed change, and we appreciate that, is to improve the healing activity of SPOZs, and consequently increase citizens' access to medical services. Thank you very much.

The populist argument in the project, which probably led to the authors of these records, is the argument about the robbery privatization of hospitals. However, so far this kind of situation has not occurred, and in turn it allows privatization where it is beneficial for the State Treasury. We support strengthening the cooperation of medical entities with the police in the search for missing persons. Undoubtedly, among these are a large group of people who have gone missing due to a rapid deterioration in their mental state, especially the ever increasing population of the elderly. However, it is necessary to clarify which data and in what form the medicinal product is to be provided. This should be harmonised across the country. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u727	A declaration by the Members on the 100th anniversary of the founding of the Florian Union. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 2016 marks the 100th anniversary of the founding of the Florian Union. As a long-standing member of the Board of the District Association of Volunteers of the Republic of Poland in Będzin, actively supporting people who, with their hard work, risk their lives, bring help to others, I would like to say a few words about the circumstances of this relationship and its importance for the creation of existing structures for voluntary firefighters. During World War I, so in times and circumstances very difficult, the Florian Society was formed. The Russian authorities wanted to gain the kindness of Poles by agreeing to his appointment. In August 1915 the situation on the war front changed. The Russians left Warsaw and the city was occupied by the Germans. The authorities of this latter country, as before the Russians, in an attempt to win the favor of Poles, gave their consent to a convention of fire workers. The convention took place in 1916 and one of its resolutions was the transformation of the recently established Florian Society into the Florian Union. The board was composed of: President Bolesław Chomicz, appointed Vice President Józef Tuliszkowski, Alfred Grohman acting as treasurer and many other activists who represented the Kingdom of Poland. The act of establishing the Florian Union was the culmination of the previous activities of Chomicz and Tuliszkowski, who actively supported the development of the Polish social-civil movement, which was to organize voluntary firefighters in order to better protect society from fires and disasters. The actions were carried out according to the slogan: God for glory, people for help. The main purpose of the Florian Union was to unite all fire units in Poland, as well as to focus the most outstanding activists in this field. The association of Floriański also carried out extensive training activities. Other statutory tasks of the union were among others.: setting up new guards, promoting the idea of fire insurance and building fire-resistant materials. Numerous checks have also begun to increase the efficiency of the individual's work. The Florian Union was a success. This is evidenced by the fact that in 1916 he owned several dozen guards, and in 1921. - more than 1,000 already. After Poland regained its independence in 1918, it was possible to unite the fire in the country completely. The activists of the Florian Union were the initiators of the convention, which took place in 1921. Over 3.5,000 have arrived. delegates and it was there that the Main Union of the Fire Guards of the Republic of Poland was established, which included all the voivodship unions. Bolesław Chomicz became president. One of the branches of the main association was the Florian Union, which concentrated units from Poleskie, Volynsk and Warsaw Voivodships. The Florian Union did not cease its activities until 1926. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u728	A statement of a lying article in the Gorlicka newspaper. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! One feature of journalism should be the reliable and impartial presentation of information. Political views should not affect the work of a journalist. I do not want to discuss the integrity of national media today, but I will focus on local media, in my case related to the Gorlice soil, from which I come and where I live. Friday 26 May this year. in the Gorlicka Newspaper, the author in the articles entitled: The Messengers quickly forgot about the voters?, the Messengers for six months did not find time to meet with the residents, informs their readers, among others. That since the beginning of my term of office, I've organized zero meetings. That is, of course, not true. Not only do I participate in various meetings organized by others, where we talk about the problems of particular environments, but I also organise open meetings for all residents myself. Invitations to these meetings are posted on posters, on my website, are sent to the media. In order to publicize these meetings and invite all interested residents of our district to these meetings, I even bought paid advertisements in this newspaper. It is necessary to show a lot of bad will to write that I did not organize any meeting with residents, when the last such meeting was organized on Sunday before this article and after the villages there were posters on the bulletin boards inviting us to this meeting. As in other cases, information about this meeting was also sent to the Gorlicka Gazeta. In addition, the author before writing the article was even in my office and my assistant tried to exchange meetings that I had organized and that I am preparing for the coming days. Unfortunately, none of this information is included in the article. This is not the first time that Gorlicka's Newspaper, when writing about me, misses the facts, gives inaccurate information. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As in any profession, in the profession of journalist, professional integrity is the basis of his credibility. Unfortunately, I have already noticed that I cannot count on the reliability of this politically involved editor and the objectivity of the Gorlicka Newspaper, but I am also surprised by the silence of the main publisher - the Krakow Newspaper. My assistant, who felt that her words had also been manipulated, submitted a request to correct both the main publisher to Krakow and the editor of the Gorlicka Newspaper. She wrote about the untruth of the information given. Unfortunately, according to my knowledge, no one has responded to this day.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-08-sejm-20-1.u729	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On Saturday 28 May 2016 in the Lublin architect I participated in celebrations connected with the 50th anniversary of the bishops' ordination of the Archbishop of the Archdiocese of Lublin Bolesław Pylak. On the eve of the anniversary of the ordinations, which took place on 29 May 1966 in the Lublin Cathedral, at the invitation of the Jubilee and the present pastor of Archdiocese Archbishops. Stanisława Budzik gathered representatives of the Polish Episcopate, state and local authorities and clergy, people of consecrated and faithful secular life to express their gratitude to God and the anniversary for half a century of his Bishop ministry. Archbishop Stanisław Gądecki, President of the Polish Episcopal Conference, Metropolitan of Poznań, and Archbishop Józef Michalik, Senior Archdiocese of Przemysl, presided over the solemn Holy Mass. The state of health of the birthday girl did not allow him to participate personally in the celebrations held in the archicathedral, but he joined the participants of the meeting through media broadcasting the anniversary celebrations. Earlier, the Archbishop also prepared his message, which was recorded and presented in the architect. The film was prepared with the help of TVP Lublin. The last word of the liturgy became a blessing addressed to the venerable Jubilee by Pope Francis, read by Archbishop. Stanisław Budzik, Lublin Metropolitan. High Izbo! Archbishop of Senior Bolesław Pylak was born on 20 August 1921 in Łopiennik Górny near Krasnegostaw. After graduating from primary school in his hometown, he went to Tarnowskie Góra, where he attended junior high school and high school. He was an externalist during World War II. In 1943, he joined the Lublin Seminary, which was located at the time in the Jarej Czerwnica. On 29 June 1948 he was ordained a priest and began his work as a vicar of the parish of Nałęczów. In 1949 he began his studies at the Faculty of Theology at KUL, completed with a PhD in dogmatic theology (1953). In 1952 he started working at KUL as a junior assistant at the cathedral of dogmatic theology at the Faculty of Theology, simultaneously conducting classes at the Lublin Seminary. Since 1959, he has been the vicar of the seminary of the clergyman. In 1961 he became a canonary of the cathedral chapter in Lublin. On 14 March 1966, he was appointed the title bishop of Midiki and the Lublin suffragan. Consecrated in the Lublin Cathedral on 29 May 1966 by bishops. Piotr Kałwa (co-consecrated by the bishops Ignacy Tokarczuk and Jan Mazur). In his duties, Bishop, he continued his scientific work. In 1969, he habilized in dogmatic theology. After being nominated as a docent, he became head of the cathedral of dogmatic theology. Author of book positions, a series of trials and articles. Since 1978 he has been an extraordinary professor and since 1988 he has been a professor of theology. In July 1974, after the death of Bishop. Piotr Kalwa was elected Vicar of the Chapter. On 27 June 1975 he was appointed Bishop of Lublin and on 20 July 1975 he took over the diocese. On 25 March 1992, the Archbishop of Lublin metropolitan was raised to the dignity of the Archbishop of Lublin. On 29 June 1992 he received a metropolitan palieusz from the Holy Father John Paul II, and on 2 July 1992 he solemnly took over the office of the Archbishop of the metropolitan. During his Bishop's ministry, Fr. Archbishop Bolesław Pylak ordained nearly 700 priests. At that time 141 new parishes and 400 new temples were created, and the number of participants in Sunday Mass increased from 20% in 1975 to 35% in 1996. Similarly, the number of members of holy communion has increased. As required by law, he resigned from office after age 75 in 1996. On 14 June 1997, he was released from his duties and transferred to his retirement status. Your Excellency, the Most Honourable Archbishop of the Jubilee! From here, I send my heartfelt wishes. A lot of health and all necessary graces. May the Lord God support and bless. God bless you!
